P1 high open AI001

Dynamic Python code execution

1 occurrence(s) · Suggested owner: Agent/tool developer

Why this matters

If external or generated content reaches this expression, it could execute Python with the process identity and access its files, credentials, and network.

Immediate action

Trace the input to eval/exec and replace it with fixed operations; if generated execution is essential, isolate it before accepting untrusted input.

Evidence context: source

Source evidence: deployment reachability and active use have not been established.

Affected locations (1)
  • lib/crewai/src/crewai/flow/runtime/_actions.py:309

Evidence: 79fe0cdc01a7851eca229b09

Related controls: EXEC-02

Additional defenses to verify: Enforce action policy outside the model • Isolate tool execution

Additional layers that can lower risk (2)

Enforce action policy outside the model

How it lowers risk: Have an execution service allow only named operations, permitted argument values, and caller-owned target resources before invoking a tool.

Evidence to verify: Exercise disallowed operations, additional arguments, cross-tenant identifiers, and direct calls that bypass the agent planner.

Residual limitation: Authorized operations can still be harmful when business constraints are incomplete; prompt instructions cannot enforce this boundary.

Verification status: proposed_not_verified

Related controls: AGT-01, AUTH-02, MCP-02

Guidance sources
  • MCP-TOOLS / MCP: Tools
  • OWASP-AGENT-CS / AI Agent Security Cheat Sheet

Isolate tool execution

How it lowers risk: Run the risky tool in a separate, disposable identity with no host mounts, ambient credentials, or unnecessary outbound access.

Evidence to verify: In a test environment, attempt reads outside the workspace, forbidden network connections, and resource exhaustion; retain policy and denial evidence.

Residual limitation: Isolation limits reachable assets; it does not make injected code trustworthy or rule out a runtime escape.

Verification status: proposed_not_verified

Related controls: EXEC-02, SUP-05, OPS-02

Guidance sources
  • JOINT-DEPLOY / Deploying AI Systems Securely: Best Practices for Deploying Secure and Resilient AI Systems
Guidance sources (5)

P1 high open AI005

Executable deserialization requires trusted inputs

1 occurrence(s) · Suggested owner: Application/model pipeline owner

Why this matters

If an attacker can supply or replace the serialized input, loading it may execute code even before the application examines the returned object.

Immediate action

Establish who can produce and replace the input; migrate to a nonexecutable data format or restrict verified legacy artifacts to an isolated conversion path.

Evidence context: source

Source evidence: deployment reachability and active use have not been established.

Affected locations (1)
  • lib/crewai/src/crewai/utilities/file_handler.py:166

Evidence: 3f3582d92e63364d20ae13d3

Related controls: EXEC-06

Additional defenses to verify: Verify artifact identity before use • Separate artifact conversion from production

Additional layers that can lower risk (2)

Verify artifact identity before use

How it lowers risk: Require reviewed artifact provenance plus a digest or signature checked against an independently trusted identity or manifest.

Evidence to verify: Substitute bytes, producer identity, or verification metadata in a controlled test and confirm the consumer rejects the artifact.

Residual limitation: Authenticity establishes the producer and bytes; an approved producer can still ship vulnerable or malicious content.

Verification status: proposed_not_verified

Related controls: SUP-03, DATA-04

Guidance sources
  • OPENSSF-MODEL-SIGNING / Launch of Model Signing v1.0
  • SLSA-12 / Supply-chain Levels for Software Artifacts specification

Separate artifact conversion from production

How it lowers risk: Perform necessary conversion of legacy serialized artifacts in a disposable environment without production data or credentials.

Evidence to verify: Demonstrate that a conversion job cannot reach production services and only approved output formats are promoted.

Residual limitation: Conversion output still needs integrity, format, and downstream behavior review; isolation is not proof of benign content.

Verification status: proposed_not_verified

Related controls: EXEC-06, SUP-06

Guidance sources
  • JOINT-DEPLOY / Deploying AI Systems Securely: Best Practices for Deploying Secure and Resilient AI Systems
  • TECH-PYTORCH-SERIAL / PyTorch Serialization semantics
Guidance sources (6)
Guidance sources

P1 high open AI010

Credential-like literal in source or configuration

21 occurrence(s) · Suggested owner: Credential/service owner

Why this matters

If the detected literal is a live credential, anyone with access to the source, image layers, or copied artifacts may use its granted permissions.

Immediate action

Determine whether the value is real without reproducing it; revoke or rotate a real exposed credential and remove retained copies through the incident process.

Evidence context: source

Source evidence: deployment reachability and active use have not been established.

Evidence: 27c4bdaae215550927b730ae, 9cb95c0ae62712ab0b25af56, 03069aa0f0b7755c360776b2, 42b6334d75c3841a3177e38f, f94ddfd7070ae6062c6520e0, b1ad520e337242b37a2142fa, 618cfc06b2b80698c3949796, e4660cb2ac019a73596542f5, 5a4e61e7d189255221135b22, 11afe2a4c39db184aeb86216, ef165bb2704953926c7774b1, 9c51c200c7f8eabc394c88e2, fe88374b37fabda0254dd409, c36cc7796b9efc531cde6184, bf5abd839b9c322d119de7be, d1d4da20406596fc58d3a078, 4aaab87ae3c2d02524e33f15, e14a24e8a2e7cfe9b081560c, ce3a76857d4291cf95072f17, 0463a5facfb23c815036d41e, 13f0e8f8fbfab9dfb07a9c26

Related controls: AUTH-05, DATA-01

Additional defenses to verify: Investigate and contain confirmed secret exposure • Use managed credential injection and revocation • Limit credentials available to the workload

Additional layers that can lower risk (3)

Investigate and contain confirmed secret exposure

How it lowers risk: For a real exposed credential, identify reachable systems, revoke or rotate it, and review access records for misuse.

Evidence to verify: Confirm the old credential no longer works and record the exposure window, affected copies, and follow-up owner.

Residual limitation: Successful rotation stops future use of that credential but cannot reverse access or data loss that already occurred.

Verification status: proposed_not_verified

Related controls: AUTH-05, OPS-06

Guidance sources
  • CISA-JCDC / JCDC AI Cybersecurity Collaboration Playbook and Fact Sheet (release notice)
  • NIST-SSDF / Secure Software Development Framework (SSDF) Version 1.1

Use managed credential injection and revocation

How it lowers risk: Load secrets at runtime through an approved store or workload identity; keep them out of source, artifacts, and model context.

Evidence to verify: Test rotation and revocation without disclosing values; inspect build layers and launch metadata for retained copies.

Residual limitation: Deleting a file or hiding a later image layer does not revoke a credential or erase copies already distributed.

Verification status: proposed_not_verified

Related controls: AUTH-05, DATA-01, DATA-06

Guidance sources
  • JOINT-AGENTIC / Careful adoption of agentic AI services
  • MCP-AUTH / MCP: Authorization

Limit credentials available to the workload

How it lowers risk: Give this component a distinct identity and only the downstream scopes and lifetime needed for its approved operations.

Evidence to verify: Use its runtime identity to attempt forbidden service operations, then revoke it and confirm subsequent access fails.

Residual limitation: A compromised component can still use its allowed permissions until credentials expire or revocation takes effect.

Verification status: proposed_not_verified

Related controls: AUTH-05, AUTH-09

Guidance sources
  • JOINT-AGENTIC / Careful adoption of agentic AI services

P1 high open AI017

JWT verification explicitly bypassed

1 occurrence(s) · Suggested owner: Identity/API owner

Why this matters

If unverified claims authorize a request, a caller may forge identity or privileges without possessing a valid issuer-signed token.

Immediate action

Verify signatures and an explicit algorithm allowlist, trusted issuer, intended audience, expiry, and required claims before authorization.

Evidence context: source

Source evidence: deployment reachability and active use have not been established.

Affected locations (1)
  • lib/crewai-core/src/crewai_core/auth/utils.py:34–36

Evidence: 2dccbd60402f0e027708e2f2

Related controls: AUTH-03

Additional defenses to verify: Authenticate and authorize protected requests • Authorize downstream effects separately

Additional layers that can lower risk (2)

Authenticate and authorize protected requests

How it lowers risk: Authenticate each protected request and enforce tool, tenant, and target permissions at the service handling the operation.

Evidence to verify: Test missing, expired, wrong-audience, and revoked credentials, plus a valid caller requesting a forbidden target.

Residual limitation: Authentication identifies a caller; it does not make their supplied data safe or grant every authenticated caller equal authority.

Verification status: proposed_not_verified

Related controls: AUTH-01, AUTH-02, AUTH-03

Guidance sources
  • MCP-AUTH / MCP: Authorization
  • MCP-AUTH-SEC / MCP: Authorization Security Considerations

Authorize downstream effects separately

How it lowers risk: For each effect, check caller identity, tenant, operation, and target at the executing service using bounded downstream credentials.

Evidence to verify: Try read-only users against writes, other-tenant identifiers, and direct tool calls without an agent session.

Residual limitation: Correct token verification alone cannot prevent an authorized but overly broad or harmful business operation.

Verification status: proposed_not_verified

Related controls: AUTH-02, AUTH-04, AGT-01

Guidance sources
  • CSA-AGENT-IAM / Agentic AI Identity and Access Management: A New Approach
  • MCP-TOOLS / MCP: Tools
Guidance sources (5)
Guidance sources
  • CSA-AGENT-IAM / Agentic AI Identity and Access Management: A New Approach
  • MCP-AUTH / MCP: Authorization
  • MCP-AUTH-SEC / MCP: Authorization Security Considerations
  • MCP-SECURITY / MCP Security Best Practices
  • MCP-TOOLS / MCP: Tools
P1 high open AI036

SQL query constructed with interpolation

2 occurrence(s) · Suggested owner: Database/tool developer

Why this matters

If untrusted values are inserted as SQL syntax, they may alter a query to read or change unintended records within the database identity authority.

Immediate action

Bind values as parameters and allowlist any dynamic identifiers or operation choices; trace generated and tool-supplied query fragments.

Evidence context: source

Source evidence: deployment reachability and active use have not been established.

Affected locations (2)
  • lib/crewai-tools/src/crewai_tools/tools/singlestore_search_tool/singlestore_search_tool.py:309
  • lib/crewai/src/crewai/memory/storage/kickoff_task_outputs_storage.py:163

Evidence: 85adbedb5e4226054387556e, 1c257f99645334875880b6ee

Related controls: EXEC-03

Additional defenses to verify: Constrain the database identity and operation set • Authorize downstream effects separately

Additional layers that can lower risk (2)

Constrain the database identity and operation set

How it lowers risk: Use dedicated database credentials with only the required tables, rows, and operations; separate administrative access from agent tools.

Evidence to verify: Using the agent identity, attempt schema changes, other-tenant records, unrestricted exports, and unauthorized writes.

Residual limitation: Database permissions bound damage but cannot make a permitted query correct; query parameters do not safely bind identifiers.

Verification status: proposed_not_verified

Related controls: AUTH-02, EXEC-03

Guidance sources
  • CISA-SECURE-BY-DESIGN / Shifting the Balance of Cybersecurity Risk: Principles and Approaches for Secure by Design Software
  • JOINT-AGENTIC / Careful adoption of agentic AI services

Authorize downstream effects separately

How it lowers risk: For each effect, check caller identity, tenant, operation, and target at the executing service using bounded downstream credentials.

Evidence to verify: Try read-only users against writes, other-tenant identifiers, and direct tool calls without an agent session.

Residual limitation: Correct token verification alone cannot prevent an authorized but overly broad or harmful business operation.

Verification status: proposed_not_verified

Related controls: AUTH-02, AUTH-04, AGT-01

Guidance sources
  • CSA-AGENT-IAM / Agentic AI Identity and Access Management: A New Approach
  • MCP-TOOLS / MCP: Tools

P2 medium open AI040

Unsafe HTML rendering boundary

2 occurrence(s) · Suggested owner: Frontend/rendering owner

Why this matters

If unsanitized agent or tool content reaches a raw HTML sink, attacker-controlled markup may execute in a viewer browser and access that viewer session.

Immediate action

Render external output as text or apply a maintained, context-appropriate sanitizer before the sink; verify the final rendering path.

Evidence context: source

Source evidence: deployment reachability and active use have not been established.

Affected locations (2)
  • lib/crewai/src/crewai/flow/visualization/assets/interactive.js:1467
  • lib/crewai/src/crewai/flow/visualization/assets/interactive.js:2449

Evidence: 8b3833f27d56aa534cae0980, 44de396274469bfe8faf7373

Related controls: EXEC-07

Additional defenses to verify: Apply browser defenses around rendered output • Isolate untrusted previews from privileged sessions

Additional layers that can lower risk (2)

Apply browser defenses around rendered output

How it lowers risk: Render data as text by default; where HTML is essential, use a maintained sanitizer and a restrictive Content Security Policy.

Evidence to verify: Exercise active markup, event handlers, URL schemes, and sanitizer bypass regressions in the actual rendering component.

Residual limitation: CSP is an additional layer and can be weakened by unsafe directives; sanitization must match the eventual HTML or URL context.

Verification status: proposed_not_verified

Related controls: EXEC-07, TEST-06

Guidance sources
  • OWASP-OUTPUT2025 / LLM05:2025 Improper Output Handling

Isolate untrusted previews from privileged sessions

How it lowers risk: Place intentionally active external previews on a separate origin or an appropriately restricted sandbox, without application credentials or privileged messaging capabilities.

Evidence to verify: Attempt parent-frame access, navigation, storage access, and privileged postMessage calls from the preview; confirm explicit rejection.

Residual limitation: Sandbox permissions can restore dangerous authority, and origin isolation does not make downloaded content safe to execute elsewhere.

Verification status: proposed_not_verified

Related controls: EXEC-07, AUTH-02, TEST-06

Guidance sources
  • OWASP-OUTPUT2025 / LLM05:2025 Improper Output Handling
Guidance sources (2)
Guidance sources
  • OWASP-LLM2026 / OWASP GenAI LLM Top 10 2026
  • OWASP-OUTPUT2025 / LLM05:2025 Improper Output Handling

highopenAI001

Dynamic Python code execution

lib/crewai/src/crewai/flow/runtime/_actions.py:309–309

Finding ID: 79fe0cdc01a7851eca229b09
Confidence: medium · Category: tool_execution

eval or exec receives a nonliteral expression. If agent, user, or tool content can reach it, it can execute arbitrary Python. Static analysis does not establish the full data flow.

Observed evidence

        exec(compile(module, filename, "exec"), namespace)  # nosec B102 # noqa: S102

highopenAI005

Executable deserialization requires trusted inputs

lib/crewai/src/crewai/utilities/file_handler.py:166–166

Finding ID: 3f3582d92e63364d20ae13d3
Confidence: medium · Category: deserialization

Pickle-compatible deserialization can execute code. This finding identifies a dangerous trust boundary, not proof that the serialized input is attacker controlled.

Observed evidence

                    return pickle.load(file)  # noqa: S301

Remediation: Use a nonexecutable serialization format with schema validation. If compatibility requires pickle, accept only authenticated artifacts from a strictly controlled producer.

Weakness mappings: CWE-502

Finding references
  • https://docs.python.org/3/library/security_warnings.html
mediumopenAI040

Unsafe HTML rendering boundary

lib/crewai/src/crewai/flow/visualization/assets/interactive.js:1467–1467

Finding ID: 8b3833f27d56aa534cae0980
Confidence: medium · Category: output_handling

Dynamic content is passed to an HTML rendering bypass. Agent or tool-generated content may contain executable browser markup if not sanitized.

Observed evidence

    this.elements.content.innerHTML = content;

Remediation: Render untrusted output as text or sanitize with a maintained HTML policy before using a raw-HTML sink; apply a restrictive content security policy.

Weakness mappings: CWE-79

Finding references
  • https://genai.owasp.org/resource/owasp-genai-llm-top-10-2026/

Separate advisory layer

Optional security review

Model output is nondeterministic and advisory. It cannot dismiss deterministic findings, change their severity, establish compliance, or change the deterministic CI gate.

Disabled. No optional LLM request was made. The deterministic scan and this report run independently.

Complete control checklist

Controls and acceptance checks

Back to top

All 66 catalog controls appear below. Expand a control for acceptance checks, source guidance, mapped findings, and any optional analyst review.

These are project-defined checks mapped to published guidance, not official benchmark scores. no_pattern_detected means only that the mapped detector did not fire. findings_detected requires investigation; it is not an automatic compliance failure.

GOV-01Inventory every agent, MCP server, tool, and modelmanual_review_required

Category: Governance · Validation: manual

Not established by this static scan

Acceptance checks

  1. Record owner, deployment, model/version, MCP transport/version, exposed tools, data classes, and external endpoints.
  2. Reconcile approved inventory with deployed configurations; investigate unregistered agents and servers.

Source mappings: NIST-RMF

Additional alignment: CSA-AICM, CSA-AISMM, CIS-AGENTS-2026, CIS-MCP-2026

Published control guidance
  • https://www.nist.gov/publications/artificial-intelligence-risk-management-framework-ai-rmf-10
GOV-02Model trust boundaries and attack pathsmanual_review_required

Category: Governance · Validation: manual

Not established by this static scan

Acceptance checks

  1. Diagram user, model, memory, tool, server, gateway, and downstream service boundaries with their credentials.
  2. Identify who controls each input and the highest-impact action reachable if that input is malicious.

Source mappings: MITRE-ATLAS, NIST-AML

Additional alignment: CSA-MAESTRO, MITRE-ATLAS-202609

Published control guidance
  • https://github.com/mitre-atlas/atlas-data
  • https://csrc.nist.gov/pubs/ai/100/2/e2025/final
GOV-03Define authorized use and accountable ownersmanual_review_required

Category: Governance · Validation: manual

Not established by this static scan

Acceptance checks

  1. Assign an owner to each autonomous action and document permitted purposes, forbidden outcomes, and escalation routes.
  2. Record impact, reversibility, approval requirements, and accepted residual risk before production use.

Source mappings: NIST-RMF, JOINT-AGENTIC

Additional alignment: CSA-AICM, CSA-SCOPING, CSA-AISMM, AIUC1-Q3-2026

Published control guidance
  • https://www.nist.gov/publications/artificial-intelligence-risk-management-framework-ai-rmf-10
  • https://www.cyber.gov.au/business-government/secure-design/artificial-intelligence/careful-adoption-of-agentic-ai-services
GOV-04Maintain evidence and risk exceptionsmanual_review_required

Category: Governance · Validation: manual

Not established by this static scan

Acceptance checks

  1. Give each control an owner, evidence link, validation date, result, and next review date.
  2. Time-limit exceptions and require a compensating control; distinguish untested behavior from demonstrated failure.

Source mappings: NIST-GENAI, NIST-SSDF

Additional alignment: CSA-AICM, CSA-AISMM, NIST-SSDF-AI

Published control guidance
  • https://nvlpubs.nist.gov/nistpubs/ai/NIST.AI.600-1.pdf
  • https://csrc.nist.gov/pubs/sp/800/218/final
GOV-05Reassess changes in autonomy and integrationsmanual_review_required

Category: Governance · Validation: manual

Not established by this static scan

Acceptance checks

  1. Review security impact when adding a model, tool, server, data source, skill, or broader permission.
  2. Require deployment evidence for the complete configured system; a model-only score is insufficient.

Source mappings: NCSC-SECURE-AI

Additional alignment: CSA-AICM, CSA-SCOPING

Published control guidance
  • https://www.ncsc.gov.uk/collection/guidelines-secure-ai-system-development
GOV-06Assign shared security responsibilities across AI providersmanual_review_required

Category: Governance · Validation: manual

Not established by this static scan

Acceptance checks

  1. For each model, gateway, orchestrator, MCP service, and cloud provider, document which party implements each applicable safeguard and which customer configuration it depends on.
  2. Obtain current supplier evidence for inherited safeguards, identify unowned gaps, and record reassessment triggers in the service review.

Source mappings: CSA-AICM, CSA-CCM

Published control guidance
  • https://cloudsecurityalliance.org/artifacts/ai-controls-matrix-v1-1
  • https://cloudsecurityalliance.org/artifacts/cloud-controls-matrix-v4-1
AUTH-01Authenticate protected operations on every requestno_pattern_detected

Category: Identity and authorization · Validation: hybrid

Partial static coverage only; absence of a finding is not a pass

Acceptance checks

  1. Trace authentication to every protected HTTP entry point, including tool calls, subscriptions, and retries.
  2. Verify missing, expired, revoked, or malformed credentials cannot invoke a protected operation.

Partial static rules: AI026, AI041

Source mappings: MCP-AUTH

Additional alignment: CSA-AGENT-IAM, CIS-MCP-2026, OWASP-AISVS-C10

AUTH-02Authorize the exact action and targetno_pattern_detected

Category: Identity and authorization · Validation: hybrid

Partial static coverage only; absence of a finding is not a pass

Acceptance checks

  1. Check user/agent identity, tenant, tool, target object, and requested operation immediately before execution.
  2. Deny by default; test read-only callers against write tools and object identifiers owned by another user.

Partial static rules: AI027

Source mappings: JOINT-AGENTIC, MCP-TOOLS

Additional alignment: CSA-AICM, CSA-AGENT-IAM, CSA-SCOPING, CIS-MCP-2026, OWASP-AISVS-C9, OWASP-AISVS-C10

AUTH-03Validate access-token cryptography and claimsfindings_detected

Category: Identity and authorization · Validation: hybrid

Partial static coverage only; absence of a finding is not a pass

Acceptance checks

  1. Verify signature with trusted keys, allowed algorithms, expected issuer/audience, expiry, and required scopes.
  2. Reject unsigned tokens and tokens minted for another service; decoding a JWT alone is not validation.

Partial static rules: AI017

AUTH-04Prevent token passthrough and confused deputy useno_pattern_detected

Category: Identity and authorization · Validation: hybrid

Partial static coverage only; absence of a finding is not a pass

Acceptance checks

  1. Use audience-bound MCP credentials and separately authorized downstream credentials; never forward arbitrary caller tokens.
  2. Ensure a proxy cannot use its broader service identity to perform an action the caller cannot authorize.

Acceptance checks

  1. Use short-lived scoped credentials where supported, protect refresh tokens, and validate rotation and revocation.
  2. Avoid tokens in query strings, model context, source, child-process arguments, and diagnostic output.

Not established by this static scan

Acceptance checks
